Lose the way so arrival is welcome [Head 259–263] top.
- **Melody** - Questioning leaps, light pulse, moderate tempo, sparse (inf.). Collectible fragments that each play part of one tune, so finds assemble it [Summers 193–195] conv.
- **Harmony** - IV ↔ V shuttle in parallel [TLTT 560–561] [T]. "Strange ground": vi, chains of 6/3 chords, displaced accents, home by fifths [Zbikowski 151–157] top. The find: a root-position bVI [Lehman 177–178] conv.; awe chains past wonders [Lehman 192–197] conv.
- **Colours and devices** - *Mystery and investigation*'s light pulse, *Dungeon ambience*, *Wonder and discovery* at the find. A magic place sighted: static harp octaves, slow low woodwind chords, then brass [Brill 27] conv.; shimmering brass and string figures as a wonder appears [Brill 34] conv. A unique three-note celesta or glockenspiel chime near something important [Summers 118] conv. World map topic (T177).
- **With / against** - With mystery, the search; wonder, the find; the underground, the delve.
- **Intensity** - Hint: one questioning leap or the chime. Whole: light pulse with a wonder swell at the find.
- **Not** - Constant events (§7.6 r16); music that steers or comments [Summers 104–106] conv.

#### Thresholds (beginnings and endings)
- **Cues** - Time codes (about to, has just) rise with non-recurring themes, harmonic blur, orchestral and tempo changes and one-way sweeps (VVA 021–033; TLTT App. 5.3 [T]). Episodic markers: one-second run-ups, propulsive motifs, unrevoked accelerando or crescendo, breaks; finality markers; an open tail (MM 516–522); cancelling a marker stops it pointing forward [TLTT 102] top. "Has just happened": modest if known, darker if not [TLTT 130–131] top. Fanfares and rockets open, hunt calls close, unstable topics sit in the middle [Monelle 9] top.; military themes open, drones close [Galand 456] conv. Session start: one fixed 5–10 s sting every time [Summers 15–16] conv.; campaign opening: the main theme in full [Summers 16–17] conv.
- **Melody** - Direct openings: coups d'archet or a unison motto; indirect: a sunrise or hymn chords [Sisman 108–109] top. Overture: tonic pedal and drum bass while the tune climbs in a crescendo [Sutcliffe 136–137] top. A rapid upward run into a downbeat [TLTT 447] conv. Two lines parting by semitones as a crossing [Brill 25, 27] conv.
- **Harmony** - Endings: at rest (plagal onto the first root tonic, motif an octave up); by force (falling la-pentatonic tutti); doom (all stops but one low note); open (end on bIII or the loop's least stable chord) [TLTT 536–537, 630–631, 660–664; Lehman 263 n42] conv. (inf.). Deflation close: ff V – I, then a soft unison falling arpeggio [Sutcliffe 137–138] top. Seams: a clean switch = rupture, a hidden pivot = magic passage [Lehman 218–219] conv. A lament bass at the start is unstable, unless harmonised as a tonic prolongation [Caplin 419–440] top.
- **Colours and devices** - Session opening: theme head after a run-up; a cliff-hanger: timpani roll into an add9 chord and stop (Kojak §6.1.3.5). Head-turning opening: quiet high dry pulses, then a low boom [TLTT 644–651] conv. A one-way harp sweep a bar before the change [TLTT 386–387] (inf.); a new solo timbre at a return = time has passed [TLTT 434, 471] [T]. Ballad frame: bare octaves and harp around a told story [Dickensheets 133, 136] top.; declamatory bridge between scenes [Dickensheets 113] top. New era: two snares in unison and a soft cymbal [Summers 120] conv. Score the glimpse of another world before anyone crosses [Binns 134] conv. A last call echoed softly as a tail [Monelle 52] (inf.).
- **With / against** - The marker says when, the concept says what. Cut at the next phrase or bar junction; hard cuts expose seams [Summers 73–80] conv.; place one-off flourishes on the beat [Summers 76] conv. Ombra introduction blown away by the Allegro [McClelland 295] top.
- **Intensity** - Hint: a one-second run-up. Whole: a short cue (marker, statement, tail), or full "once upon a time" frames [Binns 143] conv.
- **Not** - A marker that rises and falls straight back: markers are one-way.

#### Intrigue and farce
*New (from the Handbook): the comic scene of several plans colliding, the heist going sideways, the masked ball in the wrong rooms. Not Cunning (one deceiver) or Chaos (disorder).*
- **Cues** - The imbroglio topic: intrigue, confusion, running about [Sisman 90–91] top. Soft dynamics broken by timpani-backed tutti outbursts [Sisman 90–91] top.; sudden dynamic or texture changes with no structural reason [Hunter 79–82] top. Several characters at once, each in their own music [Hunter 65–66] top.
- **Melody** - Quick, syncopated, detached; a small upbeat figure after a drop to *p* [Sisman 90–91; Hunter 62–64] top. Short scraps hocketed between groups [Hunter 79–82] top. 2/4 or 4/4 allegro, major (inf.).
- **Harmony** - Light galant harmony, the bass held back and entering later to steady things [Sisman 90–91] top.
- **Colours and devices** - The motive passed among oboe, flutes and bassoons over light pizzicato, with timpani and tutti stabs [Sisman 90–91] top., scoring (inf.). Models: the *Figaro* overture, the Prague Symphony finale [Sisman 90–91] conv.
- **With / against** - With the pastoral, a flute countermelody moves the farce into the green world [Sisman 90–91] top. With the party, each plotter's motif as one of the tossed scraps [Hunter 65–66] (inf.). With crime, the heist gone wrong.
- **Intensity** - Hint: one *p* detached figure tossed from oboe to bassoons. Whole: an imbroglio finale (inf.).
- **Not** - Tempesta or real menace: the imbroglio is quick and quiet [Sisman 90–91] top.

#### Growth and coming into one's power
*New (merged from the Halfyard "apprentice" and Summers "growth and advancement" proposals): a character gaining power or level, and the hand-over of agency from mentor to pupil. Not Triumph (a victory) or Heroism (the finished hero).*
- **Cues** - Growth is fuller scoring and a more complete tune, not a new tune [Summers 96] conv.; growth reads as inheriting a motif [Halfyard Apprentice 228–229] conv. The agency motif passes from mentor to apprentice as they accept their power, and becomes the drum ostinato under the power theme in the final battle [Halfyard Apprentice 228–229] conv.
- **Melody** - Three tiers: a 2–3 note fragment on one instrument, the full phrase with a small section, the complete tune with full orchestra [Summers 95–96] conv. Gains rise: a rising fourth for a small step, a rising sequence for a power-up [Summers 193] conv.; new pitches added to a caster's vocabulary [Summers 82] conv. An agency motif from the destiny theme's rise-and-fall minor-third cell [Halfyard Apprentice 226–229] conv.
- **Harmony** - The destiny theme in minor; the agency motif inherits it [Halfyard Apprentice 228] conv. Fragment over one held chord, full phrase with its cadence, complete tune with its whole progression: closure grows with the character (inf.).
- **Colours and devices** - Horns and strings for the destiny theme; taiko or timpani for the agency rhythm at the finale (inf.). Milestone: two snares in unison and a soft cymbal [Summers 120] conv.; a level-up stinger tuned to the current harmony [Summers 23, 193] conv.
- **With / against** - With wisdom, the mentor's death sets the stakes and frees the pupil [Stephen 62] conv. With mischief, untamed power as a scherzo adding layers [Halfyard Apprentice 219–220] conv. The motif can pass to an unwilling heroine at her moment [Halfyard Apprentice 228] conv. Reverse: Decline and fading, the anthem fragmenting (inf.).
- **Intensity** - Hint: a rising-fourth stinger or the agency motif once in the PC's instrument. Strong: the theme up a tier, or the motif taken over from the mentor. Whole: the first complete full-orchestra statement, or the power theme over the agency ostinato [Summers 96, 193] conv.
- **Not** - A fanfare for every small step [Summers 23] conv. Rewriting the theme or giving the grown hero a brand-new one [Summers 170; Halfyard Apprentice 228–229] conv.

## 3. Fantasy peoples and places: quick index

Start here for a people or a place, then look up each concept above. **Carry** owns the dials; **Strong** and **Colour** layer on (section 1, step 2). Topic numbers point to `src-topics.md`; devices are in `devices-clean.md`. Distinguish peoples by **harmony system and invented timbre pairing**, not by real-world ethnic instruments (R12). These are starting points; the individual character's concepts still decide.

| People or place | Carry | Strong / colour | Topics | Devices | Watch for |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Elves** (high, ancient) | Age and antiquity, or Beauty | Decline and fading; Wonder (chromatic-mediant chains); Grace and elegance | T169, T156, T057 | *Sylvan and elven*, *Ethereal Choir* | Rone's reading of Shore: elves get chromatic mediants, men modal music, small folk diatonic. Avoid a borrowed real scale as "elvish". |
| **Wood elves**, rangers | The forest | Grace and elegance; Nature and wildness; The hunt | T178, T002, T001 | *Avian Flute* | Dorian, high and low with an empty middle. |
| **Dwarves** | Earth and stone | Industry, work and machines (the forge as labour); Age and antiquity; Toughness and grit; Loyalty and honour | T061, T016, T109 | *Under the mountain*, *The place's own rhythm* | Keep the forge in square four-bar labour rhythm and modal or major; 5/4 metal ostinati are the evil-industry code (T170). |
| **Halflings**, small rural folk | Home and hearth | The rural and pastoral; Playfulness; Revelry and drink | T167, T011, T016, T060, T064 | *The tavern* | Three settings of one homeland tune: pensive, dance, hymn (T167). |
| **Gnomes**, tinkerers | Playfulness | Industry, work and machines (quartal gadgets); Mischief | T109 (light), T044 | *Mickey Mousing*, *Tick Tock Tune* | Quartal harmony reads as technical cleverness (Tagg Kojak §6.3). |
| **Orcs**, hordes | Savagery and brutality | War; Rage; Menace for a chieftain | T161 | *Drumbeat of Chaos*, *Power* | Irregular low-brass stabs and uneven drums; never a real people's drums or chant (T171, T079). |
| **Goblins**, kobolds | Mischief | The comic and the absurd; Agitation and alarm | T044, T054 | *Sneaking* | Buffa patter in minor for a nasty edge. |
| **Giants**, trolls, ogres | Weight | Power; The monster; Playfulness (a gentle one) or The comic and the absurd (a blustering one) | T026 | *Mock melodrama* | §7.6 r3: a foreign topic over rage makes a comic ogre. |
| **The fey**, the Feywild | The fey | Beauty; Ecstasy and abandon (the revel); Mischief; The uncanny (glamour) | T091, T069, T012, T066 | *Dream and hallucination*, *Ethereal Choir* | Elfin scherzo for sprites; a minor scherzo for malice; tarantella for a revel you cannot leave. |
| **The undead**, crypts | Undeath | Death; Dread; Decay | T027, T090, T005 (ghost march), T101 | *Dance of the bones*, *The haunting*, *Doom* | Ombra (trombones, diminished sevenths) for the solemn dead; danse macabre for the dancing dead. |
| **Vampires**, decadent courts | Nobility | Undeath; Temptation and seduction; Cruelty | T066 (decadent waltz), T057 (minuet as parody) | *Façade*, *Seduction* | The dance over a drone (R4). |
| **Dragons** | The monster | The sublime (the reveal); Fire; Sky, air and flight; Greed (the hoard); Stature / scale (great or cosmic) | T026, T164 | *Wonder and discovery*, *The climax* | One grand climax (R5); a hoard is a small miser's figure in a vast space. |
| **Demons**, devils, warlock patrons | The infernal and demonic | Temptation and seduction (the pact); Evil and villainy | T089 | *The infernal*, *Creepy jazz* | Tritones, chained diminished sevenths, mocking parody. |
| **Celestials**, angels, gods | The divine | Holiness; Purity; Stature / scale | T022, T087, T088 | *Cherubic Choir*, *Ethereal Choir* | A brass chorale is divine intervention (T022). |
| **Aberrations**, the far realm | The alien | The uncanny; Menace | T153, T154 | *The other-worldly glide* | No cadence. |
| **Constructs**, golems | Industry, work and machines | Weight; Loyalty and honour (the faithful golem) | T109 | *Tick Tock Tune* | Choppy, angular gestures (Tagg MM ch. 13). |
| **Horse-lords**, nomads, cavalry | Riders and the horse | Vastness and open country; Pride; Chivalry and knighthood | T010, T168, T187 | *The north* (for a northern horse culture) | Solo violin over an open-fifth drone for the Hardanger fiddle. |
| **The north**, frost peoples | Ice and cold | Toughness and grit; Legend and lore (sagas); Riders and the horse | T174, T168, T094 | *The north* | No drums, heavy reverb, metallic glints (T174); an invented mode, not a pipe-and-snap cliché. |
| **The desert**, its peoples | The desert and the wastes | Vastness and open country; Dawn, daylight and sun (glare) | T187 | *Desert* | The Hijaz-as-desert code is a 19th-century stereotype (R12). |
| **The sea**, sailors, pirates | The sea and waters | Freedom; Revelry and drink; The journey and the road | T099, T105, T059 | *Under the sea*, *Accordion to Most Sailors*, *Merry in Minor Key* | Water has no hard downbeat; sailors have a jig. |
| **A city** | The city | Wealth and luxury or Poverty by quarter; Crime and the underworld at night | T176 | *The place's own rhythm*, *The hour strikes*, *Musette* | No busy loop under long table talk. |
| **A village** | The rural and pastoral | Home and hearth; Contentment and ease | T011, T015, T064, T080 | *The tavern* | Drone under a simple tune (Tagg's pastoral synecdoche). |
| **A temple**, monastery | Holiness | Devotion; Ritual and ceremony; Solemnity and gravity | T022, T110, T087, T101, T082 | *Holy Pipe Organ*, *Funeral hymn* | Ominous chant (T159) is a dark rite, not a holy one. |
| **A dungeon** | The underground | Exploration and discovery; Dread; Mystery | T179 | *Dungeon ambience* | No events during long exploration. |
| **The underdark** | The underground | The alien; Menace; Fear | T179, T104 (octatonic) | *Drone of Dread*, *The other-worldly glide* | Echo and tight figures for tunnels, wide reverb for caverns. |
| **The court** | Nobility | Royalty and majesty; Cunning and deceit (intrigue); Grace and elegance | T057, T024, T065, T008 | *The court dance*, *Façade* | Rank by metre (R9). |
| **A tavern** | Revelry and drink | Joy; The dance; Legend and lore (the bard) | T059, T064, T016, T044 | *The tavern*, *The dreadful bard* | A small band, not the full orchestra. |
| **A forest** | The forest | Nature and wildness; The hunt by day; The fey or Dread by night | T178, T001, T002, T018 | *Sylvan and elven*, *Deep jungle* | Night horn for the enchanted wood. |
| **Mountains**, passes | Mountains and heights | The sublime; Adversity and struggle | T002 (alphorn) | *Under the mountain* (within) | Echoed horn calls, wide spacing. |
| **The planes** | The heavens and the planes | Each plane by its element or realm: Fire, The sea and waters, Sky, air and flight, Earth and stone, The infernal and demonic, The divine, The fey, The alien | T153 | *Ethereal Choir* | Give each plane its own harmony system (R12). |
| **The Shadowfell**, death-realms | Despair | Undeath; Night and darkness; Decline and fading | T086, T027 | *Drone of Dread* | Aeolian shuttle in low strings (Tagg MM ch. 9). |
| **Ruins** | Decay | Age and antiquity; Decline and fading; Memory and nostalgia | T072, T095 | *Requiem for a Landmark* | Play the old theme whole first, then let it wear. |
| **A wizard's tower**, library | The arcane | Wisdom; Magic; Mystery | T019, T037, T104 | *Spellcraft*, *Mystery and investigation* | Fugato for study, whole-tone for spells. |
| **A thieves' guild** | Crime and the underworld | Cunning and deceit; Secrecy | T160 | *Creepy jazz*, *Sneaking* | Keep the tritone and drop the jazz timbres in a medieval idiom. |
| **A battlefield** | War | Combat and the duel; The army and the soldier; Desperation and the last stand | T161, T005 | *The great battle*, *Encounter battle music* | A war comes in waves. |
| **A graveyard**, tomb | Death | Undeath; Solemnity and gravity | T007, T101, T058 | *Funeral hymn*, *Doom* | Low bells, a sarabande's weight. |
| **A frontier town** | Vastness and open country | The city (small); Toughness and grit | T150, T151, T152 | *The road* | Open fifths and a bVII-V. |
| **A ship** | The sea and waters | The journey and the road; Freedom | T099, T059 | *Accordion to Most Sailors* | Rocking 6/8. |
| **A safe haven**, inn after battle | Sanctuary and safety | Rest and sleep; Healing; Home and hearth | T098, T011 | *Lullaby* | Nothing sudden (§7.6 r16). |

## 4. Worked method

**A village priest** (kind, plain-spoken, loved by the village).
- Concepts: devotion (carrying; religious mode, R9), humility (strong), the rural and pastoral (colour), tenderness (colour), Stature: chamber.
- Dials: slow (about 66), soft, major, small dynamic range, legato: devotion, humility and tenderness agree (R1).
- Texture: their tune recast as a plain chorale, a chord on every beat in soft strings with a quiet organ underneath. The tune itself is on oboe, which gives the village's pastoral colour instead of grandeur; a soft cello drone under the first phrase is the pastoral's genre synecdoche (R10).
- Moments: a plagal "amen" closes each phrase, and one soft bell sounds at the end. The peak is a modest swell with the horns doubling, and no trumpets, choir mass or timpani.

**A feral druid of the deep woods** (half-wild, old as the trees, gentle with beasts and savage to intruders).
- Concepts: the forest (carrying), age and antiquity (strong), the storm (strong, at one moment), loneliness (colour), Stature: regional.
- Dials: moderate tempo, Dorian (the forest topic, T178), mid-to-low register with an empty middle.
- Texture: polyrhythmic toms and timpani (3 against 2, soft) under a drone of open fifths in the cellos and basses. The tune is on horns with wide leaps, and avian flute figures answer it high.
- The gentle/savage conflict is split by layer, then by moment (R4): birdsong high, drums low. The storm crack comes once, as a short tempesta (T028): timpani rolls, diminished-seventh tremolo, and the tune in octaves on low brass. It then falls back to a lone flute.
- Avoid harp arpeggios and pretty Lydian chords (*Sylvan and elven* is too refined for a feral figure).

**A cowardly but lucky merchant** (talks big, flinches at everything, always gets away).
- Concepts: cowardice (carrying; comic mode), luck (strong), the far-away (colour: their trade route), pride (colour: the sales pitch), Stature: chamber.
- Dials: quick (about 112), light, major. The tune is in staccato bassoon and clarinet over a pizzicato tiptoe, with a dash of *Musette* (clarinets in thirds) for the foreign goods.
- Moments: a brassy *Showman's* pickup for the boast, then a scurrying run away from it.
- Fear and luck alternate by moment: each near-disaster builds (tremolo, a held dominant), then swerves to a bright chord in an unexpected key (the cadence that lands far away, T156) with a glockenspiel sparkle. The piece ends on a comic V-I button, never a grand climax.

**A fading elven queen** (ancient, gracious, grieving for a people that is leaving the world; still terrible when roused).
- Concepts: decline and fading (carrying; tragic mode in its slow, high form, R9), beauty (strong), the sublime (strong, at one moment), grace and elegance (colour), Stature: great.
- Dials: slow (about 60), soft to moderate, a key that never settles for long: chains of chromatic-mediant chords (Rone's elvish harmony system, R12) rather than a borrowed exotic scale.
- Texture: a long arching line on solo violin over string pads and harp arpeggios (Tagg's tactile luxury and romance, without percussion); high choir enters on the second statement. Each phrase ends on a fall, Gondor-in-Decline fashion.
- Signs (R10): the pad is a tactile anaphone, the falling cadences a kinetic one, the harp a bardic synecdoche of the ancient; a one-second rising run-up (episodic marker) announces the moment she is roused.
- The one climax (R5): a hush, then a sudden tutti on a remote major chord with brass in unison, the sublime's terror (T026), which falls back at once to the solo violin. The last chord is an open Iadd9 (*Farewell*).

## Source key

Strength marks: [S]/[M]/[W] research strength (research.md); [T] Tagg listener data; [ev] other listener evidence; top. documented topic; conv. convention; (inf.) inference; [gen.] unchecked.

- **Groups ABC:** Strength marks: [S]/[M]/[W] research strength; [T] Tagg listener data; [ev] other listener evidence; top. documented topic; conv. convention; (inf.) inference; [gen.] unchecked. Codes such as §1, R11, T022, VVA, HEV, SW, LOTR are the lexicon's existing citations. Short page citations: Dick. = Dickensheets, Topical Vocabulary; Lehman = Hollywood Harmony; Monelle = The Musical Topic; Summers = Game Music; Tagg = Tagg (& Clarida), Ten Little Title Tunes. Film-music volume chapters: Barron (Legend), Binns (Scissorhands), Brill (Harryhausen), Halfyard (Apprentice), Halfyard & Hancock (Indy/Mummy), Hayward (Last Wave), Murphy (Final Fantasy), Stephen (Queen), Webster (Potter), Winters (Superman). Numbers after a name are pages.
